Task ID: 39-idle-auto-logout
Agent: Z.ai Code (main orchestrator)
Task: 5-MINUTE INACTIVITY AUTO-LOGOUT (trace 1a0bc6a406866b5d) — backend-authoritative idle session timeout, frontend warning at T-30s, cross-tab sync, PWA-safe; background polling/heartbeat/WS must NOT extend the session; active users must NEVER be logged out.

Work Log:
- RECON COMPLETE (existing auth architecture — extend, never replace):
  * Sessions are DB-backed opaque tokens (Session table: token unique, expiresAt, ip, userAgent, lastSeenAt) in HttpOnly SameSite=Lax cookie; getSessionUser() (lib/hms/auth.ts, server-only) is THE single funnel for every authenticated request via handler() (lib/hms/api.ts) → this is where the idle check belongs.
  * EXISTING behavior: sliding renewal extends expiresAt when < half TTL remains (on EVERY request incl. background polls — that's the 7-day ABSOLUTE cap, fine to keep); NO idle/inactivity concept exists today → a user stays signed in indefinitely while any traffic flows. Root cause of current behavior: lastSeenAt updates on every request but nothing enforces inactivity.
  * Client: session.tsx SessionProvider (mounted in app/[[...slug]]/page.tsx above Gate+Shell) holds user state, silent 4-min GET /auth/session heartbeat, refresh(), signOut() → POST /auth/logout (existing, audited LOGOUT). Auth screens are the Gate at / with ?auth=login (AuthFlow; AuthLoginScreen takes an oauthError banner prop — a session-expired notice can ride the same rail).
  * Realtime: RealtimeProvider disconnects the WebSocket when user goes null (existing cleanup covers §9/§15 presence). api-client throws ClientApiError{code,status} — no central 401 handler yet.
  * KEY ARCHITECTURAL FACT (drives the design): the server cannot observe user interaction; per spec §1/§8/§25 background requests must NOT count as activity → lastActivityAt can ONLY be advanced by an explicit client report driven by REAL user events (throttled), while every other request (polling, heartbeat, WS) leaves it untouched. The server stays authoritative: it validates idle on EVERY request and invalidates when the threshold passes, regardless of what the client does.
- DESIGN (implemented):
  * CONFIG: SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS env (default 300, production value; automated tests run the server with 5). Central definition in auth.ts (SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS); exposed to the client once via GET /auth/session payload (idleTimeoutSeconds) — no hardcoded 300 anywhere else.
  * SCHEMA: Session.lastActivityAt DateTime @default(now()) — the ONLY idle clock; login sets it (default); ONLY the activity endpoint advances it; sliding renewal keeps touching expiresAt/lastSeenAt only.
  * BACKEND ENFORCEMENT (getSessionUser): after the absolute-expiry check → idle = now - lastActivityAt; if idle >= SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS → delete session, audit SESSION_EXPIRED_IDLE_TIMEOUT (metadata: idleSeconds, email — no tokens), record rejection reason, return null → handler() answers 401 with code SESSION_EXPIRED (module-level reason channel consumed by handler; generic 401s unchanged). Lazy enforcement = the standard session architecture (checked on every authenticated request, exactly per spec §3 flow).
  * ACTIVITY REPORT (NEW POST /api/v1/auth/activity): advances lastActivityAt=now; auth goes through the same handler/getSessionUser path. Driven ONLY by the client's real-event listeners, throttled to one ping per ~20s — background polling NEVER calls it.
  * LOGOUT (existing POST /auth/logout REUSED): accepts optional {reason:"idle"} from the idle flow → audits SESSION_EXPIRED_IDLE_TIMEOUT (alongside the existing LOGOUT audit) — no new endpoint.
  * FRONTEND (NEW components/hms/idle-session-guard.tsx mounted in shell.tsx): wall-clock state machine (1s tick + visibilitychange) reading idleTimeoutSeconds from the session context — PWA/background-safe (no reliance on background timers firing). Activity signals: pointermove/click/keydown/touchstart/wheel/scroll (passive, throttled) + popstate + the router's ROUTE_EVENT (navigation). warnAt = min(30s, 0.4×timeout) → 4:30 warning at 300s, 2s at the 5s test value. Warning dialog: "Your session will expire in N seconds due to inactivity." + [Continue Session] (REAL interaction: resets the clock + immediate activity ping) + [Logout Now] (existing logout). At 5min of NO interaction: POST /auth/logout{reason:idle} → sessionStorage notice → BroadcastChannel("hms-session-bus") "expired" → full state clean via provider → window.location.assign("/?auth=login"). Other tabs receive the broadcast → identical cleanup (§10). Server-side SESSION_EXPIRED 401 on any API call also routes into the same cleanup via a central listener in SessionProvider ("hms:session-expired" event dispatched ONCE from api-client on code SESSION_EXPIRED — no per-call duplication, §17).
  * LOGIN NOTICE: auth-flow reads+closes the sessionStorage notice and passes it to AuthLoginScreen (new notice prop, same banner rail as oauthError): "Your session has expired due to inactivity. Please log in again."
  * UNSAVED WORK (§14): the existing useDraft autosave keeps drafts across the reload; the T-30s warning is the protection window; Continue Session never destroys state.
- IMPLEMENTED:
  * SCHEMA: Session.lastActivityAt DateTime @default(now()) — the single idle clock. db:push + generate clean. (Redis untouched — no Redis session store exists in this architecture; PostgreSQL remains authoritative.)
  * BACKEND (lib/hms/auth.ts): SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS env (default 300; tests run the server with 5) → SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS; module-level rejection-reason channel consumed by handler(); getSessionUser() now enforces INACTIVITY after the absolute-expiry check: idle = now − lastActivityAt; idle ≥ timeout → session row DELETED + audit SESSION_EXPIRED_IDLE_TIMEOUT (idleSeconds/threshold/email — no tokens) + reason=IDLE_TIMEOUT; handler() (lib/hms/api.ts) then answers 401 code SESSION_EXPIRED ("Your session has expired due to inactivity. Please log in again.") instead of the generic UNAUTHORIZED. Sliding renewal still only touches expiresAt/lastSeenAt — NEVER lastActivityAt (§8). createSession stamps lastActivityAt=now.
  * NEW POST /api/v1/auth/activity: advances lastActivityAt scoped to THIS session token (token+userId where-clause — activity in one device never extends another device's session); goes through the same handler/getSessionUser funnel so an already-crossed-threshold session is rejected 401 SESSION_EXPIRED. This is the ONLY endpoint that can move the idle clock.
  * EXISTING POST /auth/logout REUSED (§18): optional {reason:"idle"} → additional SESSION_EXPIRED_IDLE_TIMEOUT audit (initiatedBy:client) alongside the existing LOGOUT audit.
  * SESSION PAYLOAD: GET /auth/session now returns idleTimeoutSeconds (server config → client UX timers; single source, nothing hardcoded client-side).
  * FRONTEND: api-client dispatches ONE "hms:session-expired" window event on any 401 SESSION_EXPIRED (central interception §17 — no per-call logic); SessionProvider listens: sessionStorage notice + clearLastRoute + setUser(null) (RealtimeProvider then disconnects WS/presence via its existing user-null path §9/§15) + location.assign("/?auth=login"); NEW components/hms/idle-session-guard.tsx mounted in shell.tsx — wall-clock 1s state machine (background-timer-throttling-proof, PWA-safe) + visibilitychange re-evaluation; activity = pointermove/pointerdown/click/keydown/touchstart/wheel/scroll (passive+capture) + popstate + the router's ROUTE_EVENT (navigation); activity REPORTS throttled to ≤1/20s (no per-mousemove requests §4); warning at min(30s, 0.4×timeout) → 4:30 at 300s with a live seconds countdown + [Continue Session] (REAL interaction: resets the local clock + immediate forced activity ping — nothing automatic §5) + [Logout Now] (existing logout); at the deadline: POST /auth/logout{reason:"idle"} → sessionStorage notice → BroadcastChannel("hms-session-bus") "expired" → signOut + redirect (§6). Other tabs receive the broadcast and sign out WITHOUT calling the server again (§10). AuthLoginScreen shows the one-shot amber banner "Your session has expired due to inactivity. Please log in again." via a lazily-consumed sessionStorage flag (consumed only when the login form actually mounts — a race found in QA where a transient auth-flow mount swallowed the notice was fixed).
  * UNSAVED WORK (§14): the T-30s warning dialog is the protection window + existing useDraft autosave keeps drafts; dialog copy explicitly tells the user drafts are preserved.
- QA scripts/idle-qa.ts (12 checks, ALL PASS; server run with SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=5): config exposure; activity endpoint advances the DB clock (scoped); 9+s of REPORTED activity → session alive; the EXISTING heartbeat pattern (GET /auth/session ×3 across the window) does NOT keep the session alive → row DELETED; protected API at the threshold → 401 SESSION_EXPIRED + row gone + SESSION_EXPIRED_IDLE_TIMEOUT audit (no secrets); dead token → 401.
- BROWSER QA (agent-browser, PRODUCTION 300s value — real elapsed waits): login → NO interaction → warning dialog appeared at ~4:30–4:46 with live countdown (screenshot) → deadline reached → auto-logout → /?auth=login with the amber inactivity banner (verified) + server audit row; second run: warning countdown=11 → [Continue Session] clicked → dialog closed, stayed on dashboard → 45s later (past the original deadline) session STILL authenticated (server clock was moved by the real interaction); multi-tab: tab A idle vs tab B kept locally active by a synthetic-event interval → at expiry TAB-A logged out with banner AND TAB-B logged out too via the broadcast (locally-active tab cannot outlive the session); ACTIVE-USER test: pointer+click every 5s for 5.5 MINUTES (110% of the timeout) → never logged out, never warned, authenticated=true at the end. Mobile (390px) layout of the dialog verified in screenshots.
- BUILD: full-repo `bun run lint` exit 0; tsc — zero new errors (4 pre-existing in other agents' files remain); dev.log clean; dev server restarted WITHOUT the env override → session payload reports idleTimeoutSeconds=300 (production configuration restored, §21).
- FILES: NEW src/app/api/v1/auth/activity/route.ts, src/components/hms/idle-session-guard.tsx, scripts/idle-qa.ts. EDITED: prisma/schema.prisma (Session.lastActivityAt), src/lib/hms/auth.ts, src/lib/hms/api.ts, src/lib/hms/api-client.ts, src/components/hms/session.tsx, src/components/hms/shell.tsx, src/app/api/v1/auth/session/route.ts, src/app/api/v1/auth/logout/route.ts, src/components/hms/auth/auth-flow.tsx, src/components/hms/auth/login-screen.tsx.

Stage Summary:
- The app now auto-logs-out after exactly 5 minutes of NO genuine user interaction, with the SERVER as the final authority (Session.lastActivityAt enforced in getSessionUser on every authenticated request; 401 SESSION_EXPIRED; audited). Background polling, the 4-minute session heartbeat, WebSocket heartbeats and token refresh cannot extend a session because the only clock-mover is the throttled activity endpoint fed exclusively by real UI events. Active users are never logged out (verified for 5.5 continuous minutes), the T-30s warning offers a real Continue Session, cross-tab logout is broadcast, the PWA path is wall-clock-safe with server re-validation, and production remains at 300 seconds (automated tests may run 5 via env). Logout reuses the existing endpoint; no new session architecture, database, or realtime system was created.

Task ID: whatsapp-device-linking-fix
Agent: Z.ai Code (main orchestrator)
Task: Fix WhatsApp device linking / "No device found" (trace 1a0bf518da853661) — inspect real architecture, trace the full pairing flow, fix the root cause without duplicating any system

Work Log:
- RECON (§1): mapped the REAL stack — OpenWA gateway (pinned rmyndharis/OpenWA v0.23.5, Baileys 7.0.0-rc14 engine, port 2785, git-ignored clone) + ONE WhatsAppService (src/lib/hms/whatsapp/*) + admin APIs + Settings→WhatsApp UI. No FastAPI/Redis/second session system exists or was added. Gateway was NOT deployed in this sandbox (clone absent) — deployed it fresh: clone at pinned commit, npm ci, nest build, .env (PORT=2785, ENGINE_TYPE=baileys, API_MASTER_KEY, sqlite, SSRF_ALLOWED_HOSTS=127.0.0.1), data reset once because first boot seeded a random key before the env existed.
- LIVE TRACE (§2/§7/§24): engine connects to WhatsApp servers (web.whatsapp.com 200, ws/chat reachable, TLS clock sane, dynamic 5-tier WA-Web version resolver — no stale pin). Observed REAL lifecycle in logs: QR generated → rotates every ~20s (md5 changes) → unscanned windows end with Baileys close 408 "QR refs attempts ended" → status INITIALIZING → reconnect with capped backoff → fresh QR. Pairing-code endpoint issues real 8-char codes (3 issued: 3C216DSN, DMPXNLS3 + one more). No protocol incompatibility found.
- ROOT CAUSE (§14): "No device found" = the mobile scanned a DEAD QR ref. Chain: (a) the gateway engine caches the LAST rendered QR through transient socket drops (this.qrCode is NOT cleared on close) — during the reconnect backoff (up to 60s+/attempt) GET /sessions/{id}/qr still answers 200 with that stale PNG plus status:initializing; (b) our getSessionQrDataUrl() served ANY non-empty QR and ignored the status field; (c) the frontend polled the QR every 20s (a full rotation period) and rendered whatever came back, no age signal, red-error treatment for benign windows. Additionally captured live: a QR served UNCHANGED for 45+s on a live socket between rotations.
- FIX (backend, our layer only — pinned gateway untouched): getSessionQrDataUrl now serves a QR ONLY when the engine status is qr_ready (freshness gate; falls back to the just-synced live status), maps initializing/created + the gateway's first-QR 400 "not ready yet" to a typed WAITING state, everything else to UNAVAILABLE; the /qr route returns {qr:null,state:WAITING} instead of throwing for waiting windows. getSessionPairingCode syncs status first (refuses ready sessions, honest message when engine re-establishing), and maps Baileys' closing-socket pairing refusal to actionable recovery text. connectSession is now IDEMPOTENT (§16): reuses an already-active session (initializing/qr_ready/authenticating/ready) instead of re-starting (gateway 400 "already started") and no longer stamps `initializing` over a live session's true status.
- FIX (frontend, whatsapp-tab.tsx): QR poll 20s→10s (≤ one rotation), new typed states — amber "Waiting for a fresh QR" spinner panel (WAITING) vs red error panel; live QR age line ("QR is fresh — scan it now · fetched Ns ago"); §15 recovery buttons Refresh QR / Refresh pairing (existing reconnect stop+start → immediate fresh socket) / Use pairing code (cross-link to the pairing dialog); explicit "No device found" troubleshooting hint. Status mapping already honest (mapSessionStatusToUi, live probe per §6/§21) — unchanged.
- QA: NEW scripts/whatsapp-pairing-qa.ts (13/13 PASS, real gateway+app): idempotent connect, fresh-only QR guarantee, honest WAITING windows, status mirror equals gateway truth (no fake states), pairing code issued through the app, disconnect→honest DISCONNECTED→QR refused→reconnect restored. Existing scripts/whatsapp-qa.ts 49/50 (only "webhook registered" needs a genuinely linked phone — registration fires on ready, impossible pre-link; delivery pipeline itself green: idempotency, complaint E2E, RBAC, secrets). Fixed two QA-script flakes: async-audit race (+1.5s wait) and a naive "apikey"-substring false positive (flag NAME apiKeyChanged:true is not a secret — now asserts no secret VALUES appear). Browser QA (agent-browser): admin→Settings→WhatsApp→Connection; QR dialog shows fresh QR + age; Refresh pairing → WAITING panel → auto-recovery to fresh QR; pairing code dialog issues DMPXNLS3; mobile 390+320px no horizontal overflow, dialog 288px at 320. Lint + tsc clean, no dev.log errors.
- HONEST LIMIT (§25): an actual mobile scan cannot be performed from this sandbox (no physical handset). Everything up to the scan is verified live: live socket, rotating fresh QRs, pairing codes issued, honest states, recovery loop. The user must complete the final scan on their phone; with the fix, a scanned QR is guaranteed ≤ ~10s old and the UI no longer offers dead codes.

Stage Summary:
- Root cause of "No device found": stale dead QR refs served (gateway caches the last QR through socket drops; app served it blindly; UI polled too slowly and showed it without age/state context) — users scanned expired codes. Fixed in OUR integration layer only (service.ts freshness gate + idempotent connect, qr route typed WAITING, QR dialog honest states + recovery actions + 10s poll); pinned OpenWA gateway untouched, zero new systems. 13/13 pairing QA + 49/50 full QA + browser E2E green. Ops note: gateway deploy = clone pinned commit + npm ci + nest build + .env (see mini-services/WHATSAPP-GATEWAY.md); first boot seeds API_MASTER_KEY (or generates + stores data/.api-key).

Agent: Z.ai Code (main orchestrator)
Task: Build a complete Firebase Cloud Messaging (FCM) push notification system integrated into the existing notification architecture (trace 1a0bf7e7de672de5); no duplicate architecture; real end-to-end delivery required before claiming success

Work Log:
- RECON (§1): mapped the existing system end-to-end — ONE NotificationService (notify()/notifyRole() in services.ts, ~30 call sites across complaints/WO/PM/invoices/IRMS/HR/checklist/whatsapp-inbound/workflow handlers), Notification table (in-app), PushSubscription (VAPID web-push via push-server.ts), ONE service worker (public/sw.js with push + notificationclick routing), DomainEvent outbox + scheduler workers (email/whatsapp pattern), automation toggle push_notifications ("Requires Firebase configuration" hint already in UI), RBAC via PERMISSIONS, session auth via handler(), rate-limit + audit helpers. NO Firebase/FCM existed anywhere; no Redis client in the app (queues are DB-backed outbox loops).
- DESIGN (§2/§10/§36): FCM added as a TRANSPORT inside the existing pipeline: notify() → sendPushToUser() (public API unchanged) → enqueuePush (PushLog QUEUED, idempotent by dedupeKey = notificationId) → push/worker.ts in the EXISTING scheduler loop → FCM (when configured + automation toggle on) and/or legacy VAPID (preserved unchanged) → device. Zero new tables for notifications; zero second systems.
- DB (§6/§27/§19): prisma +PushDevice (FCM registry, token unique, installationId, failure bookkeeping), +PushLog (EmailLog-pattern delivery history: QUEUED→SENDING→SENT/FAILED/retry→DEAD_LETTER + SKIPPED with errorCode Unconfigured/NoDevices/ChannelDisabled, per-recipient counts, fcmMessageId, targetDeviceId, isTest), +NotificationPreference (per-user category map). db:push clean.
- BACKEND (§4/§21-§23/§29/§37): src/lib/hms/push/ domain — fcm.ts (lazy Admin SDK init, sendEachForMulticast 500-token chunks, per-token sent/permanent/transient classification, data-only messages + Urgency headers), vapid.ts (idempotent setVapidDetails — fixes a REAL defect: the scheduler bundle's web-push instance was unconfigured → sends without Authorization → 401), preferences.ts (10 categories + resourceType mapping), worker.ts (atomic claim, stuck-SENDING recovery, backoff 30s/2m/10m max 3, DEAD_LETTER, device deactivation on permanent errors, daily cleanup: failureCount>=5 devices + 90d PushLog retention; in-app notifications never touched). services.ts notify() now links notificationId + priority (ERROR/WARNING→HIGH). scheduler.ts hooks tickPushWorker (no second scheduler). serverExternalPackages: web-push+firebase-admin in next.config.ts.
- APIS (§7/§8/§30/§31): /api/v1/push/status extended (fcm block with public client config served to AUTHENTICATED clients — rotation without rebuild), devices/register (upsert by token, session-derived user, rate limit, audit), devices/unregister, devices GET/DELETE (merged FCM+VAPID list, ownership-checked idempotent remove), preferences GET/PUT, admin/overview (push.view), admin/devices, admin/test (push.manage; inline worker execution returns the AUTHORITATIVE result — "Success" only when the transport accepted; audited + isTest).
- FRONTEND (§3/§4/§16/§18/§32/§34): push-client.ts (lazy firebase imports, server-provided config, getToken bound to the EXISTING /sw.js registration — NO competing firebase-messaging-sw; foreground onMessage = refresh nudge only, no duplicate OS notification; syncPushOnLogin silent re-bind when permission already granted). pwa.ts enablePush = ONE flow: FCM if configured else VAPID; disablePush both. sw.js v2→v3: safePayload unwraps the FCM data-envelope (existing push handler displays + routes; click routing untouched). pwa-menu.tsx §18 permission dialog (explain → Enable/Not Now; denied → browser-settings hint, no auto re-prompt). Profile: push-settings.tsx (Registered devices + Push preferences cards). Settings: notifications-tab.tsx admin center (honest channel status + setup guidance, stats 7d, recent deliveries/errors, test sender user→device→real result). session.tsx: signOut unregisters this browser's FCM token (device/session policy §34); VAPID keeps its existing owner-rebinding policy.
- RBAC (§12/§25/§35): +push.view/push.manage permissions (ADMIN roles inherit); recipients resolved from canonical DB at event time (role change = immediate eligibility change); routes server-computed + relative-only (SW rejects absolutes); no client-trusted ids anywhere.
- TESTING (§40/§41) — real, not mocked: Chromium new-headless + persistent context (old headless shell always denies notifications; ephemeral contexts = incognito → Push API disabled — both discovered and worked around), real permission grant, real pushManager.subscribe to Google's endpoint, device row persisted, REAL business event (complaint created) → push RECEIVED + DISPLAYED by the browser SW with app icon + deep-link /complaints/{id} (relative, RBAC-safe), BACKGROUND delivery with no tab open, admin test sender → SENT 2/2, Google FCM accepted a real message (HTTP 201 + message id) which the live browser displayed, retry system proven in real conditions (transient 502 → backoff → SENT), honest negatives (SKIPPED/NoDevices, register refused without Firebase, fcm.configured:false surfaced everywhere), preferences round-trip, duplicate suppression, lint clean, regressions green (login/complaints/dashboard/notifications/email/whatsapp/push APIs all 200). Fixed during QA: Radix Select empty-value crash in the test dialog (sentinel), script bugs (201-is-success, getNotifications dict serialization).
- HONEST LIMIT (§44): the sandbox cannot create a real Firebase project (no Google Cloud access) — the FCM transport is fully implemented and activates by adding the documented credentials (FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT + FIREBASE_CLIENT_* + FIREBASE_VAPID_KEY + Automation toggle); until then every surface reports configured:false TRUTHFULLY and the VAPID transport (same pipeline/worker/SW) carries deliveries — real browser delivery was verified through it. Sandbox egress also intermittently 502s POST bodies to jmt17.google.com (Chrome's push edge) — the worker's retry handles it (proven); production (www.mohdhms.com, home server) has no such restriction. docs/PUSH-FCM-REPORT.md is the full report; .env.example documents all Firebase/VAPID env vars + console checklist.

Stage Summary:
- FCM is now the primary push transport of the ONE existing notification pipeline (notify → queue → worker → FCM/VAPID → device), fully integrated: PushDevice registry, PushLog authoritative history, per-user category preferences, §18 permission UX, profile device management, admin notification center + real test sender, login/logout device lifecycle, RBAC + idempotency + retry + cleanup. Real end-to-end push delivery verified in-browser (business event + background + test sender + retry recovery + Google 201); real-Firebase-project delivery pending ops credentials (documented, honestly surfaced as "Not configured"). No duplicate notification architecture created; existing email/WhatsApp/PWA/realtime untouched and regression-tested green.

Task ID: whatsapp-qr-fix
Agent: Z.ai Code (main orchestrator)
Task: FIX WHATSAPP QR + FULL SYSTEM AUDIT (trace 1a0c85fede796af1) — end-to-end audit of the existing OpenWA/WhatsApp architecture, find the real QR failure, fix root causes, no second system anywhere.

Work Log:
- AUDIT (§1): mapped the REAL stack end-to-end: ONE OpenWA gateway (pinned rmyndharis fork v0.23.5 commit bcd820d, Baileys 7 engine, port 2785) + ONE WhatsAppService (src/lib/hms/whatsapp/* 12 files) + /api/v1/whatsapp/* (session/qr/pairing-code/reconnect/disconnect, config, webhook HMAC, health, templates, automations, messages, conversations) + Settings→WhatsApp tab (QR dialog w/ 10s poll, age line, WAITING/ERROR states, Refresh QR / Refresh pairing / pairing-code fallback) + WhatsApp Inbox module. NO FastAPI/Redis/second session system exists or was added. Prior session's fixes (freshness gate, idempotent connect) verified intact in code.
- ROOT CAUSES FOUND (three, stacked — all environmental, code-level chain verified sound):
  1. GATEWAY ENTIRELY DOWN in this sandbox: clone (git-ignored) had been wiped AND the app's WhatsAppConfig DB row was NULL — nothing existed; Connect could only fail.
  2. WRONG ENGINE on redeploy: the doc's `.env.runtime` filename is WRONG — the gateway's ConfigModule loads `.env` from the clone root (verified in src/app.module.ts; `data/.env.generated` holds only infra defaults). Without ENGINE_TYPE the engine factory silently falls back to whatsapp-web.js → Puppeteer needs "Chrome ver 146" which is absent → every session start crashed "Could not find Chrome" → NO QR EVER (this is exactly the class of failure behind "QR displays but never pairs" on any host where wwebjs is mis-deployed). Fixed: proper `.env` with ENGINE_TYPE=baileys (per doc) — baileys needs no browser; verified `engineType=baileys` in boot log, wwebjs plugin disabled.
  3. APP COLD-BOOT BLOCKER (found while restarting the dev server): an untracked fossil `src/app/page.tsx` (left on disk when commit 3312282 moved routing to the `[[...slug]]` catch-all) collides with the catch-all at "/" — Next 16 refuses to boot ("route with the same specificity"). The long-lived dev process predated the fossil; every cold boot died. Deleted the fossil (catch-all serves "/" identically) — app boots again.
- SANDBOX INFRA RESTORED: gateway rebuilt at the pinned commit and moved OUTSIDE the app tree (/home/z/openwa) because the sandbox caps inotify at 8192 (unraisable) and OpenWA's ~77k files crashed next dev's watcher ("OS file watch limit reached"); doc updated. Gateway key adopted from its self-seeded data/.api-key into the app via the app's own PATCH /api/v1/whatsapp/config (AES-256-GCM at rest, hint-only in responses). No second architecture created.
- LIVE QR SCIENCE (§3/§4/§34): the displayed QR is a real PNG (89504e47 magic) that DECODES to `https://wa.me/settings/linked_devices#2@<ref>,<keys>,…` — the CURRENT official WhatsApp companion-device format (Baileys 7.x companion-reg-client-utils.js:32), NOT a website/dummy QR; older audits that only md5-compared PNGs missed this. Rotation is server-driven ~20-25s (observed 3 refs/50s) and can pause late in a QR window; after ~2min unscanned WhatsApp ends the QR-ref window (Baileys close 408) and the engine cycles initializing → fresh QR — the app's freshness gate (serve ONLY while engine status == qr_ready; WAITING state otherwise) + 10s dialog poll mean a displayed QR is at most ~10s old and dead codes are never offered.
- QA: NEW scripts/whatsapp-e2e.ts (permanent ops tool, lint-clean, 36 checks ALL PASS against the live stack): RBAC (401/403 ×4 surfaces), secret-write-only, honest status mirror + uiState mapping, connect idempotency (no duplicate gateway sessions — count stays 1 across re-connect/stop/reconnect/restart), QR = real PNG + genuine modern pairing ref + rotation/freshness, pairing code issued (8-char, e.g. YYCPS49C), webhook fail-closed (bad/missing signature 401) + positive signed session.status delivery (processed, row persisted, duplicate idempotent), disconnect→honest DISCONNECTED→QR refused (400, no dead QR)→reconnect→qr_ready, health payload truthful. Restart recovery: killed the whole app, booted fresh → status recovers from gateway (qr_ready), still exactly ONE session.
- BROWSER E2E (agent-browser, admin): Settings → WhatsApp → Connection → Show QR → dialog "Link a device" renders the LIVE QR (data:image/png) + "QR is fresh — scan it now · fetched Ns ago"; auto-refetch verified (age advanced, same ref while rotation paused — honest); mobile 390px: no overflow, QR 280px fully visible; console clean; screenshot /tmp/wa-qr-dialog.png.
- REGRESSION (§36): 17 API surfaces green (auth, dashboard, complaints, work orders, invoices, customers, email bootstrap, notifications, push status, whatsapp conversations/messages/templates(15)/automations(7)/meta/health, files storage, portal scoping, supervisor whatsapp view). lint exit 0; tsc zero errors in owned files (46 pre-existing in other agents' scripts/areas unchanged).
- HONEST LIMIT (§13/§35/§41.12-13): a REAL phone scan cannot be performed from this sandbox (no physical handset). Everything up to the scan is verified live and green: live Baileys socket to WhatsApp servers, genuine rotating pairing QR ≤10s old, pairing codes issued, honest states, recovery loop, exactly-one-session. The user must complete the final step on their phone: Settings → WhatsApp → Connection → Show QR → WhatsApp mobile → Linked Devices → Link a Device → scan the CURRENT QR (or use the pairing code shown by the app). On the production host, deploy per mini-services/WHATSAPP-GATEWAY.md — ENGINE_TYPE=baileys in `.env` (clone root) is the single most critical line; a "Could not find Chrome" in the gateway log means the engine fell back to whatsapp-web.js.

Stage Summary:
- The QR failure was NOT the QR image: three stacked environmental root causes (gateway+config wiped, engine silently falling back to Chrome-dependent whatsapp-web.js on the documented redeploy path, and a route-conflict fossil blocking every app cold boot) meant no live pairing session could ever exist; on hosts where an old cached QR was served the prior session's freshness gate now prevents dead codes. All fixed within the EXISTING architecture (same gateway, same service, same UI; zero new systems). 36/36 QA + browser E2E + regressions green; final phone-scan step documented for the user.

WhatsApp 36/36 regression re-passed after sandbox recovery. Lint clean.

- Results (§8/§9/§10/§14/§20/§22): groups only when non-empty (Modules / Complaints / Work Orders / Customers / Equipment — Work Orders added because the existing placeholder already promised it and its API supports ?search=); click → shell's navigateFromSearch (dirty-guard preserved) to the entity detail page; "View all results →" → new /search?q= module page (registry navHidden, no permissions → all roles reach it; groups inside permission-gated; same APIs pageSize 25; refine field re-submits via useModuleQuery apply). - RBAC/security (§21/§22): unchanged permission-gated endpoints called per user perms; backend remains authoritative (scopes resolved server-side); no sensitive-field exposure added (same API projections the modules already return).

Task ID: 16-realtime
Agent: Z.ai Code (main orchestrator)
Task: FIX AND HARDEN THE REALTIME WEBSOCKET, PRESENCE & EVENT DISPATCH SYSTEM — dashboard showed "Connected clients: 0 / Service: unreachable" with 2 devices online; trace the full chain (Browser → socket.io client → gateway → realtime-service → auth → connection manager → presence → dispatcher → PostgreSQL outbox → broadcast), fix the root cause, verify with a real two-device test.

Work Log:
- ROOT CAUSE (traced, evidence-backed):
  (1) PROCESS LAYER: the realtime-service (ports 3003/3004) and the Next.js server (3000) were BOTH dead — the kernel OOM-killer had killed next-server (14 kills on 2026-09-23, RSS 2.2–3.3 GB each on the 4 GB box; dmesg evidence). With nothing on :3004, /api/v1/realtime/health returned service:null → dashboard rendered "unreachable" + zeros (its ?? 0 fallbacks made API-failure indistinguishable from real zeros — spec §27/§31 violation).
  (2) SANDBOX OPS: setsid-launched processes are reaped between tool calls; the working pattern is the double-fork subshell `(cmd </dev/null >log 2>&1 &)` (worklog line 902).
  (3) BELL BUG: GET /api/v1/notifications never implemented its own documented meta.unread → the header badge was pinned at 0 forever, so realtime notification delivery was invisible even when events flowed.
  (4) SHUTDOWN BUG: realtime-service SIGTERM handler used httpServer.close(cb) which waits for open sockets → zombie process (accepts nothing, holds live sockets forever, never exits).
  (5) ADJACENT: email worker tick threw `ReferenceError: smtpReady is not defined` every 10s tick (variable renamed providerReady elsewhere) — same scheduler hosts the realtime dispatch loop.
- FIXES (all in existing architecture, no new systems):
  mini-services/realtime-service/index.ts — (a) session-revocation sweep: every socket's session re-verified against /api/v1/auth/session at most once/45s (15s sweep); DEFINITIVE unauthenticated → emit realtime:session-invalid + force-disconnect (single-active-device/idle/logout enforcement); transient app outage NEVER disconnects (restart tolerance §26); (b) per-user lastSeenAt tracked via socket.use middleware; (c) internal health gained status:"healthy" + lastSeen; (d) graceful shutdown: io.close() + internalServer.close() + 3s force-exit timer.
  src/lib/hms/realtime/socket.ts — client handles realtime:session-invalid: publishes DISCONNECTED and tears the singleton down cleanly (no infinite backoff loop against a dead session; next login reconnects).
  src/app/api/v1/realtime/health/route.ts — top-level status (healthy/degraded — never hardcoded), websocket, redis:"n/a (postgresql-outbox)" (spec §15 compliance: PostgreSQL stays authoritative), dispatcher running flag, connected_clients/events_broadcast/outbox_pending/awaiting_broadcast/last_broadcast_at/last_dispatch_at aliases; backward-compatible service/outbox keys; still SUPER_ADMIN-only.
  src/components/hms/modules/settings/automation-tab.tsx — dashboard honesty: health-API failure → "—" + red "diagnostics unavailable"; service down → socket metrics "—" while DB-backed outbox numbers stay real; healthy → live values; data-testids realtime-connected-clients/-events-broadcast/-service-state.
  src/app/api/v1/notifications/route.ts — implemented meta.unread (global unread count for the user; contract comment already promised it) → bell badge shows the real backlog and ticks on NOTIFICATION_CREATED.
  src/lib/hms/email/service.ts — smtpReady → providerReady (one-word ReferenceError fix in the shared scheduler loop).
  next.config.ts — experimental.turbopackMemoryLimit 1.2 GB (dev only) to stop the recurring OOM kills (default 8 GB let RSS balloon to 2.4+ GB).
- LIVE TWO-DEVICE VERIFICATION (real sockets, real events, no page reloads):
  T1 connect: browser d1=operations(ADMIN) + browser d2=admin(SUPER_ADMIN) via gateway /?XTransformPort=3003 → health: clients=2, presence [Operations Manager:1, MohdAdmin:1], Service online; header indicators "Realtime connection: Live".
  T2 d2→d1: complaint created from d2 session → d1 bell 41→42 LIVE (no reload); d2 dashboard Events 0→3 live, Last broadcast COMPLAINT_CREATED.
  T3 d1→d2: complaint created from d1 browser session → scripted supervisor socket (gateway path) received NOTIFICATION_CREATED + COMPLAINT_CREATED; d1 bell 42→43.
  T4 refresh: reload d1 → clients 1→1 (old socket closed on unload, new connected, no duplicates).
  T5 network interruption: set offline on → clients 0; offline off → auto-reconnect (socket.io backoff), clients 1, indicator Live. No duplicates.
  T6 revocation: d1's session revoked server-side (logout API with the live cookie, browser left open) → sweep logged session-revoked-disconnect within ~45s; indicator "Realtime connection: Offline" with NO reconnect loop (client teardown handler). Also organically verified: agent-browser sessions generate no real user activity → 300s idle revocation → 401 → app sign-out → socket closed (§12 chain works end-to-end).
  T7 logout: UI Sign out → clients 1→0, presence [] instantly (no dashboard refresh).
  T8 service restart: SIGTERM (exposes+fixed the shutdown bug) → service restart → browser auto-reconnected WITHOUT reload → clients settled to exactly 1 (stale engine session reaped by ping timeout ~85s — §13 detection).
  T9 RBAC + final state: ADMIN → 403 on /api/v1/realtime/health (§28); SUPER_ADMIN dashboard showed Connected clients: 2, Service: online, Online now: both users; final cross-device event CPT-2026-0044: d2 dashboard Events 0→3 LIVE, Last broadcast COMPLAINT_CREATED · 2:00 PM, d1 bell 43→44.
  REST APIs remained functional throughout (logins, complaints, health polls).
- Ops notes: services restarted via double-fork subshell; dev server pre-warmed (/,/settings) after each restart (~20s cold compile); browser keepalive (synthetic pointerdown → app's own activity endpoint) used to survive the 300s idle policy during tests; both browser sessions closed afterwards to relieve memory.

Stage Summary:
- Realtime system VERIFIED end-to-end with two simultaneous devices: real connection counts, real presence, live event delivery BOTH directions, notification bell increments live, outbox+dispatcher+service health all real (no hardcoded values anywhere).
- Root causes fixed: dead processes (OOM) + OOM itself bounded (turbopackMemoryLimit), dashboard honesty (§27/§31), session revocation enforcement on live sockets (§12) incl. clean client teardown, graceful shutdown zombie fix, notifications meta.unread contract implementation, email worker ReferenceError.
- provider = "postgresql" always: honored — the DomainEvent outbox remains the authoritative relational queue (Prisma DB; PostgreSQL in production per schema flip procedure); no Redis in this stack (health reports "n/a (postgresql-outbox)"), no business data moved to cache.
- Changed files: mini-services/realtime-service/index.ts, src/lib/hms/realtime/socket.ts, src/app/api/v1/realtime/health/route.ts, src/components/hms/modules/settings/automation-tab.tsx, src/app/api/v1/notifications/route.ts, src/lib/hms/email/service.ts, next.config.ts.
